(c) the promotion of literacy among the people using
English medium of language
(d) the introduction of scientific research and
rationalism in the traditional Indian education
The correct option is (c).
• The Wood's Despatch of 1854, often called
the "Magna Carta of English Education in
India," laid out a comprehensive plan for
education.
• While it had multiple aims, a central objective
was to create a hierarchical system of
education from primary school to university.
• The stated aim was the diffusion of European
knowledge, and it explicitly recommended
that the medium of instruction for higher
education should be English. The ultimate
goal was the promotion of literacy and
modern knowledge among the people,
primarily using the English medium of
language for higher studies, to create a class
of Indians who were "Indian in blood and
colour, but English in taste, in opinions, in
morals, and in intellect."
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q11. Assertion (A): In 1916, Maulana Mohammad Ali
and Abul Kalam Azad resigned from the Legislative
Council. Reason (R): The Rowlatt Act was passed by

the Government in spite of being opposed by all
Indian members of the Legislative Council.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not a
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (d).
• Assertion (A) is false. The events are
chronologically mismatched. Maulana
Mohammad Ali and Abul Kalam Azad were
prominent leaders of the Khilafat and NonCooperation movements, but their
resignations from legislative bodies in protest
occurred later, in the context of the NonCooperation Movement around 1920, not in
1916.
• Reason (R) is true. The Rowlatt Act was
passed in 1919, and it was indeed passed by
the Imperial Legislative Council in spite of the
unanimous opposition of all its Indian
members. This blatant disregard for Indian
opinion was a major cause of the nationwide
protests that followed.
• Since the assertion is false but the reason is a
true statement of fact, the correct option is
(d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q12. Assertion (A): Shah Alam II spent the initial
years as an Emperor far away from his capital.
Reason (R): There was always a lurking danger of
foreign invasion from the north-west frontier.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. The Mughal Emperor
Shah Alam II (reigned 1759–1806) did indeed
spend the initial years of his reign (from 1760
to 1772) as a wanderer, far away from his

•

•

capital, Delhi. He lived under the protection
of the Nawab of Awadh and later the British.
Reason (R) is false. The primary reason for his
exile was not the danger of foreign invasion
from the northwest. It was the danger posed
by powerful court factions and his own
ambitious and treacherous wazir (prime
minister), Imad-ul-Mulk, who controlled
Delhi. Shah Alam II feared for his life and
could only return to his capital in 1772 with
the help of the Marathas.
Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q13. Assertion (A): Marathas emerged as the
strongest native power in India after the decline of
Mughal empire. Reason (R): Marathas were the first
to have a clear concept of a united Indian nation.
(a) Both A and R are individually true and R is the
correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are individually true but R is not the
correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true
The correct option is (c).
• Assertion (A) is true. In the 18th century,
following the rapid decline of the Mughal
Empire after the death of Aurangzeb, the
Marathas did emerge as the strongest
indigenous power in India. They controlled
vast territories and were the primary
contenders for succeeding the Mughals as the
paramount power on the subcontinent, until
their defeat by the British.
• Reason (R) is false. The Marathas did not
have a clear concept of a united Indian
nation in the modern sense. Their political
structure was a confederacy, and their
primary goal was to establish Maratha
supremacy and extract tribute (chauth and
sardeshmukhi) from other regions. Their rule
was often seen as predatory by other Indian
powers, and they did not attempt to build a
pan-Indian national identity.

•

Since the assertion is true but the reason is
false, the correct option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2004

Q1. Consider the following statements: Some of the
main features of the Government of India Act, 1935
were the
1. Abolition of diarchy in the Governors’ provinces
2. Power of the Governors to veto legislative action
and to legislate on their own
3. Abolition of the principle of communal
representation
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 2 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is correct. The Government of
India Act, 1935, abolished the system of
diarchy (dual government) in the provinces,
which had been introduced by the 1919 Act. It
replaced it with "provincial autonomy,"
granting provinces a greater degree of selfgovernment.
• Statement 2 is correct. Despite granting
provincial autonomy, the Act vested
considerable discretionary and special
powers in the Governors. This included the
power to veto legislative action passed by
the provincial legislature and the power to
legislate on their own through ordinances,
effectively undermining the autonomy.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. The Act did not
abolish the principle of communal
representation. On the contrary, it extended
and expanded it by providing separate
electorates not only for Muslims, Sikhs, and
others but also for the Depressed Classes
(Scheduled Castes), women, and labour.
• Therefore, only statements 1 and 2 are
correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. Which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) The Constituent Assembly of India was elected by
the Provincial Assemblies in the year 1946.
(b) Jawaharlal Nehru, M.A. Jinnah and Sardar
Vallabhbhai Patel were members of the Constituent
Assembly of India
(c) The First Session of the Constituent Assembly of
India was held in January, 1947
(d) The Constitution of India was adopted on 26th
January, 1950
The correct option is (a).
• Statement (a) is correct. The members of the
Constituent Assembly of India were not
directly elected by the people. They were
indirectly elected by the members of the
newly elected Provincial Legislative
Assemblies in July-August 1946, based on the
scheme proposed by the Cabinet Mission
Plan.
• The other statements are incorrect:
o (b) M.A. Jinnah was not a member of
the Constituent Assembly of India.
After the Muslim League decided to
form a separate Constituent
Assembly for Pakistan, he and other
League members withdrew.
o (c) The first session of the
Constituent Assembly was held on
December 9, 1946, not in January
1947.
o (d) The Constitution of India was
adopted on November 26, 1949. It
came into full force on January 26,
1950.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements:
1. The First Session of the Indian National Congress
was held in Calcutta.
2. The Second Session of the Indian National Congress
was held under the Presidentship of Dadabhai
Naoroji.
3. Both Indian National Congress and Muslim League
held their sessions at Lucknow in 1916 and concluded

the Lucknow Pact.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 only
(c) 2 and 3
(d) 3 only
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The first session of
the Indian National Congress was held in
Bombay (now Mumbai) in December 1885,
not Calcutta.
• Statement 2 is correct. The second session of
the Congress was held in Calcutta in 1886,
and it was presided over by Dadabhai
Naoroji.
• Statement 3 is correct. In a landmark event,
both the Indian National Congress and the
All-India Muslim League held their annual
sessions simultaneously in Lucknow in
December 1916. It was here that they jointly
signed the historic Lucknow Pact, agreeing on
a common set of demands to present to the
British government.
• Therefore, statements 2 and 3 are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. During the Indian Freedom Struggle, who among
the following proposed that Swaraj should be
defined as complete independence free from all
foreign control?
(a) Mazharul Haque
(b) Maulana Hasrat Mohani
(c) Hakim Ajmal Khan
(d) Abul Kalam Azad
The correct option is (b).
• At the Ahmedabad session of the Indian
National Congress in 1921, the prevailing goal
was still Swaraj within the British Empire.
• However, the poet and freedom fighter
Maulana Hasrat Mohani made a radical
proposal. He moved a resolution to define
Swaraj as "Complete Independence, free
from all foreign control."
• This was one of the first times that the
demand for complete independence was

formally proposed from a major Congress
platform. Although the motion was
considered too radical at the time and was
opposed by Mahatma Gandhi, it signaled the
growing sentiment for full independence that
would be officially adopted by the Congress
in 1929.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. The Montagu-Chelmsford Report formed the
basis of :
(a) the Indian Councils Act, 1909
(b) the Government of India Act, 1919
(c) the Government of India Act, 1935
(d) the Indian Independence Act, 1947
The correct option is (b).
• The Montagu-Chelmsford Report, published
in July 1918, was a set of proposals for
constitutional reforms in India. It was
authored by Edwin Montagu, the Secretary of
State for India, and Lord Chelmsford, the
Viceroy of India.
• The recommendations of this report were
given legal form and enacted as the
Government of India Act, 1919.
• The key features of this act, such as the
introduction of Dyarchy in the provinces and
the creation of a bicameral legislature at the
center, were all based on the proposals laid
out in the Montagu-Chelmsford Report.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following Princely States of the
British rule in India:
1. Jhansi
2. Sambalpur
3. Satara
The correct chronological order in which they were
annexed by the British is:
(a) 1-2-3
(b) 1-3-2
(c) 3-2-1
(d) 3-1-2
The correct option is (c).

•

•

All three of these states were annexed by
Lord Dalhousie using his controversial
"Doctrine of Lapse." To arrange them in
chronological order of their annexation:
o 3. Satara: This was the very first state
to be annexed under the Doctrine of
Lapse in 1848.
o 2. Sambalpur: This state was annexed
in 1849.
o 1. Jhansi: The state of Jhansi was
annexed in 1853, an act that would
make its queen, Rani Lakshmibai, a
legendary figure in the Revolt of
1857.
Therefore, the correct chronological order is
3, 2, 1.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q7. The name of the famous person of India who
returned the Knighthood conferred on him by the
British Government as a token of protest against the
atrocities in Punjab in 1919 was:
(a) Tej Bahadur Sapru
(b) Ashutosh Mukherjee
(c) Rabindranath Tagore
(d) Syed Ahmad Khan
The correct option is (c).
• Following the Jallianwala Bagh massacre in
Amritsar in April 1919, there was widespread
horror and indignation across India.
• As a powerful and symbolic act of protest
against this brutal atrocity, the renowned
poet and Nobel laureate Rabindranath
Tagore renounced the Knighthood that had
been conferred upon him by the British
government in 1915.
• In his letter to the Viceroy, Lord Chelmsford,
he stated that "the time has come when
badges of honour make our shame glaring in
their incongruous context of humiliation."
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8. Which of the following pairs are correctly
matched?
List-I (Period) — List-II (Wars)
1. AD 1767-69 : First Anglo-Maratha War
2. AD 1790-92 : Third Mysore War
3. AD 1824-26 : First Anglo-Burmese War
4. AD 1845-46 : Second Sikh War
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 2 and 4
(b) 3 and 4
(c) 1 and 2
(d) 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Pair 1 is incorrect. The First Anglo-Maratha
War was fought from 1775 to 1782. The
period 1767-69 corresponds to the First
Anglo-Mysore War.
• Pair 2 is correct. The Third Anglo-Mysore
War, fought between the British and Tipu
Sultan, took place from 1790 to 1792.
• Pair 3 is correct. The First Anglo-Burmese
War occurred from 1824 to 1826, resulting in
a British victory and the Treaty of Yandabo.
• Pair 4 is incorrect. The Second Anglo-Sikh
War was fought from 1848 to 1849. The
period 1845-46 corresponds to the First
Anglo-Sikh War.
• Therefore, only pairs 2 and 3 are correctly
matched.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q9. Which one of the following pairs is not correctly
matched?
(a) Pitt’s India Act : Warren Hastings
(b) Doctrine of Lapse : Dalhousie
(c) Vernacular Press Act : Curzon
(d) Ilbert Bill : Ripon
The correct option is (c).
• This pair is not correctly matched. The
infamous Vernacular Press Act, which
severely curtailed the freedom of the Indianlanguage press, was passed in 1878 during
the viceroyalty of Lord Lytton, not Lord
Curzon.

•

The other pairs are correct:
o (a) Pitt's India Act (1784) was passed
during the tenure of Warren Hastings
as Governor-General.
o (b) The Doctrine of Lapse was
aggressively implemented by Lord
Dalhousie.
o (d) The controversial Ilbert Bill (1883)
was introduced during the viceroyalty
of Lord Ripon.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q10. Consider the following Viceroys of India during
the British rule :
1. Lord Curzon
2. Lord Chelmsford
3. Lord Hardinge
4. Lord Irwin
Which one of the following is the correct
chronological order of their tenure?
(a) 1-3-2-4
(b) 2-4-1-3
(c) 1-4-2-3
(d) 2-3-1-4
The correct option is (a).
• To arrange these Viceroys in the correct
chronological order of their tenure:
o 1. Lord Curzon: Served as Viceroy
from 1899 to 1905.
o 3. Lord Hardinge II: Served as Viceroy
from 1910 to 1916.
o 2. Lord Chelmsford: Served as Viceroy
from 1916 to 1921.
o 4. Lord Irwin: Served as Viceroy from
1926 to 1931.
• Therefore, the correct chronological order is
1, 3, 2, 4.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q11. Consider the following events during India’s
freedom struggle:
1. Chauri-Chaura Outrage
2. Minto-Morley Reforms
3. Dandi March
4. Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms

Which one of the following is the correct
chronological order of the events given above?
(a) 1-3-2-4
(b) 2-4-1-3
(c) 1-4-2-3
(d) 2-3-1-4
The correct option is (b).
• To arrange these events in the correct
chronological order:
o 2. Minto-Morley Reforms: These
reforms, enacted as the Indian
Councils Act, were introduced in
1909.
o 4. Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms:
These reforms were enacted as the
Government of India Act in 1919.
o 1. Chauri-Chaura Outrage: This
violent incident took place in
February 1922, leading to the
suspension of the Non-Cooperation
Movement.
o 3. Dandi March: This event, which
launched the Civil Disobedience
Movement, took place in March-April
1930.
• Therefore, the correct chronological order is
2, 4, 1, 3.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q12. Consider the following statements:
1. In the Third Battle of Panipat, Ahmed Shah Abdali
defeated Ibrahim Lodi.
2. Tipu Sultan was killed in the Third Anglo-Mysore
War.
3. Mir Jafar entered in a conspiracy with the English
for the defeat of Nawab Siraj-ud-daulah in the Battle
of Plassey.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 3 only
(c) 2 and 3
(d) None
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Third Battle of
Panipat (1761) was fought between Ahmed

•

•

•

Shah Abdali and the Marathas, not Ibrahim
Lodi. Ibrahim Lodi was defeated by Babur in
the First Battle of Panipat in 1526.
Statement 2 is incorrect. Tipu Sultan was
killed in the Fourth Anglo-Mysore War
(1799), not the Third. The Third AngloMysore War (1790-92) ended with the Treaty
of Seringapatam.
Statement 3 is correct. Mir Jafar, the
commander-in-chief of Nawab Siraj-uddaulah's army, entered into a secret
conspiracy with Robert Clive of the English
East India Company. His betrayal on the
battlefield was a key reason for the Nawab's
defeat in the Battle of Plassey (1757).
Therefore, only statement 3 is correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2005

Q1. Which party was founded by Subhash Chandra
Bose in the year 1939 after he broke away from the
Congress?
(a) Indian Freedom Party
(b) Azad Hind Fauj
(c) Revolutionary Front
(d) Forward Bloc
The correct option is (d).
• In 1939, Subhas Chandra Bose was re-elected
as the President of the Indian National
Congress, defeating Mahatma Gandhi's
preferred candidate, Pattabhi Sitaramayya.
However, due to ideological differences with
Gandhi and the Congress Working
Committee, he resigned from the presidency.
• Immediately after his resignation, in May
1939, he announced the formation of the
Forward Bloc as a new faction within the
Congress.
• The aim of the Forward Bloc was to
consolidate all the left-wing and radical
elements within the party to push for a more
militant and uncompromising struggle
against British rule. It later emerged as an

independent political party.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q2. Consider the following statements : The
Government of India Act, 1935 provided for
1. The provincial autonomy
2. The establishment of a Federal Court
3. All India Federation at the centre
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• The Government of India Act, 1935, was a
comprehensive piece of legislation that laid
out a new constitutional framework for India.
• Statement 1 is correct: A key feature of the
Act was the introduction of provincial
autonomy. It abolished the system of diarchy
in the provinces and granted them a greater
degree of self-government with responsible
ministries.
• Statement 2 is correct: The Act provided for
the establishment of a Federal Court to
adjudicate disputes between provinces and
between the center and the provinces, and to
interpret the constitution. This court was set
up in 1937.
• Statement 3 is correct: The Act proposed the
creation of an All-India Federation at the
centre, which would consist of the British
Indian provinces and the princely states that
chose to accede.
• Since all three statements are correct, the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements:
1. Warren Hastings was the first Governor General
who established a regular police force in India on the
British pattern.
2. A Supreme Court was established at Calcutta by the
Regulating Act, 1773.
3. The Indian Penal Code came into effect in the year

1860.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The credit for
establishing a regular, organized police force
in India on the British pattern goes to Lord
Cornwallis, not Warren Hastings. Cornwallis,
as part of his administrative reforms, created
a system of thanas (police stations) under a
daroga.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Regulating Act of
1773 did provide for the establishment of a
Supreme Court at Calcutta (Fort William).
This court was established in 1774 with Sir
Elijah Impey as its first Chief Justice.
• Statement 3 is correct. The Indian Penal Code
(IPC) was drafted by the First Law
Commission chaired by Thomas Babington
Macaulay. It was enacted in 1860 and came
into force in 1862.
• Therefore, only statements 2 and 3 are
correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q4. Which one of the following is the correct
chronological order of the battles fought in India in
the 18th Century?
(a) Battle of Wandiwash - Battle of Buxar - Battle of
Ambur - Battle of Plassey.
(b) Battle of Ambur - Battle of Plassey - Battle of
Wandiwash - Battle of Buxar.
(c) Battle of Wandiwash - Battle of Plassey - Battle of
Ambur - Battle of Buxar.
(d) Battle of Ambur - Battle of Buxar - Battle of
Wandiwash - Battle of Plassey.
The correct option is (b).
• To arrange these decisive 18th-century
battles in chronological order:
o Battle of Ambur: This battle was a key
engagement in the Second Carnatic
War and was fought in 1749.

o

•

Battle of Plassey: This pivotal battle,
which established British dominance
in Bengal, was fought on June 23,
1757.
o Battle of Wandiwash: This was the
decisive battle of the Third Carnatic
War, where the British defeated the
French. It was fought in 1760.
o Battle of Buxar: This battle, which
consolidated British control over
northern India, was fought on
October 22, 1764.
Therefore, the correct chronological
sequence is Battle of Ambur, Battle of
Plassey, Battle of Wandiwash, Battle of
Buxar.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. Which one of the following is the correct
statement?
(a) The modern Kochi was a Dutch colony till India’s
independence.
(b) The Dutch defeated the Portuguese and built Fort
Williams in the modern Kochi.
(c) The modern Kochi was first a Dutch colony before
the Portuguese took over from them.
(d) The modern Kochi never became a part of the
British colony.
The correct option is (b).
• Statement (b) is correct. The Dutch, in
alliance with the Zamorin of Calicut, defeated
the Portuguese and captured Kochi in 1663.
They then established their control and built
Fort Williams there, making Kochi their main
trading post on the Malabar Coast.
• The other statements are incorrect:
o (a) Kochi was not a Dutch colony till
independence; it was ceded to the
British in 1814.
o (c) Kochi was first a Portuguese
colony (from 1503) before the Dutch
took over.
o (d) Kochi did become part of the
British sphere of influence and was a
princely state under British

suzerainty. It willingly joined the
Indian Union in 1947.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Consider the following statements :
1. Lord Mountbatten was the Viceroy when the
Shimla Conference took place.
2. Indian Navy Revolt, 1946 took place when the
Indian sailors in the Royal Indian Navy at Bombay
and Karachi rose against the Government.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Shimla
Conference, which was convened to discuss
the Wavell Plan, took place in June-July 1945.
The Viceroy at that time was Lord Wavell, not
Lord Mountbatten. Lord Mountbatten
became Viceroy in 1947.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Royal Indian Navy
(RIN) Revolt did indeed take place in February
1946. It started among the ratings (sailors) on
the ship HMIS Talwar in Bombay over issues
of pay, food, and racial discrimination, and
quickly spread to other naval establishments,
including Karachi.
• Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q7. Which one of the following territories was not
affected by the Revolt of 1857?
(a) Jhansi
(b) Chittor
(c) Jagdishpur
(d) Lucknow
The correct option is (b).
• The Revolt of 1857 was a widespread
uprising, but its impact was largely
concentrated in northern and central India.
• Jhansi, under Rani Lakshmibai, and Lucknow,
the capital of Awadh, were major epicenters
of the revolt. Jagdishpur in Bihar was the

•

center of the rebellion led by the heroic
Kunwar Singh.
Chittor, located in the princely state of
Mewar in Rajasthan, was not directly
affected by the revolt. Most of the major
princely states, including those in Rajputana
(Rajasthan), remained loyal to the British and
did not join the uprising.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

•

•

•

Q8. Which one of the following places did Kunwar
Singh, a prominent leader of the Revolt of 1857,
belong to?
(a) Bihar
(b) Madhya Pradesh
(c) Rajasthan
(d) Uttar Pradesh
The correct option is (a).
• Kunwar Singh was one of the most
remarkable military leaders of the Revolt of
1857.
• He was an octogenarian zamindar (landlord)
of Jagdishpur, which is located in the state of
Bihar.
• Despite his advanced age, he led a prolonged
and brilliant guerrilla campaign against the
British, not only in Bihar but also in areas of
Uttar Pradesh, becoming a legendary figure
of the rebellion.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. Which one of the following pairs are correctly
matched?
Movement
Person Actively Associated With
1. Champaran - Rajendra Prasad
2. Ahmedabad Mill Workers - Morarji Desai
3. Kheda - Vallabhbhai Patel
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).

•

Pair 1 is correct. During the Champaran
Satyagraha (1917), Mahatma Gandhi was
actively assisted by a team of lawyers from
Bihar, the most prominent of whom was
Rajendra Prasad.
Pair 2 is incorrect. The Ahmedabad Mill
Strike (1918) was led by Mahatma Gandhi.
His main lieutenant in this struggle was
Anasuya Sarabhai, not Morarji Desai.
Pair 3 is correct. During the Kheda Satyagraha
(1918), Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel emerged as
one of Gandhi's most trusted and capable
followers, organizing the peasants for the notax campaign.
Therefore, pairs 1 and 3 are correctly
matched.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q10. Who among the following was not associated
with the formation of U.P. Kisan Sabha in February
1918?
(a) Indra Narain Dwivedi
(b) Gauri Shankar Misra
(c) Jawaharlal Nehru
(d) Madan Mohan Malviya
The correct option is (c).
• The U.P. Kisan Sabha was formed in February
1918 to organize the peasants of Awadh
against the oppressive practices of the
talukdars (landlords).
• It was founded through the efforts of Indra
Narain Dwivedi and Gauri Shankar Misra,
with the support of the senior nationalist
leader Madan Mohan Malviya.
• Jawaharlal Nehru became associated with the
peasant movement in Awadh later, in 1920,
when he was invited by Baba Ramchandra to
visit the villages and see the conditions of the
peasants. He was not involved in the initial
formation of the U.P. Kisan Sabha in 1918.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q11. Who among the following drafted the
resolution on fundamental rights for the Karachi
Session of Congress in 1931?

(a) Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
(b) Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru
(c) Dr. Rajendra Prasad
(d) Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
The correct option is (b).
• The Karachi session of the Indian National
Congress in 1931 is famous for its resolution
on Fundamental Rights and National
Economic Programme.
• This landmark resolution, which outlined the
Congress's vision for the social and economic
structure of a free India, was primarily
drafted by Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru.
• While the resolution was influenced by
various leaders and reflected a consensus, the
initial drafting is credited to Nehru, who was
a leading figure of the socialist-leaning wing
of the Congress.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q12. In October 1920, who of the following headed a
group of Indians gathered at Tashkent to set up a
Communist Party of India?
(a) H. K. Sarkar
(b) P. C. Joshi
(c) M. C. Chagla
(d) M. N. Roy
The correct option is (d).
• The Communist Party of India (CPI) was
founded in exile in Tashkent (then in the
Turkestan ASSR, Soviet Union) on October
17, 1920.
• This initiative was taken by a group of Indian
revolutionaries who had been inspired by the
Bolshevik Revolution.
• The central figure who headed this group and
organized the formation of the party was
Manabendra Nath Roy (M.N. Roy). He was a
key figure in the international communist
movement and was acting under the aegis of
the Communist International (Comintern).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q13. At which Congress Session was the Working
Committee authorized to launch a programme of
Civil Disobedience?
(a) Bombay
(b) Lahore
(c) Lucknow
(d) Tripuri
The correct option is (b).
• The historic Lahore session of the Indian
National Congress was held in December
1929, with Jawaharlal Nehru as the
President.
• This session is famous for passing the
landmark "Poorna Swaraj" (Complete
Independence) resolution.
• Crucially, the session also authorized the AllIndia Congress Committee (AICC) and the
Working Committee to launch a programme
of Civil Disobedience, including the nonpayment of taxes, whenever they deemed it
fit. This authorization gave Mahatma Gandhi
the mandate to launch the Dandi March and
the subsequent Civil Disobedience
Movement in 1930.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q14. Where were the Ghadar revolutionaries, who
became active during the outbreak of the World War
I, based?
(a) Central America
(b) North America
(c) West America
(d) South America
The correct option is (b).
• The Ghadar Party was a revolutionary
organization founded in 1913 with the aim of

•

•

overthrowing British rule in India through
armed struggle.
The party was founded and based among the
Indian immigrant community, primarily Sikhs
from Punjab, living on the West Coast of
North America.
Its headquarters, the Yugantar Ashram, was
located in San Francisco, USA, and it had a
strong presence in other parts of the USA and
Canada. With the outbreak of World War I,
many Ghadarites returned to India to incite a
rebellion.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q15. In which one of the following provinces was a
Congress Ministry not formed under the Act of 1935?
(a) Bihar
(b) Madras
(c) Orissa
(d) Punjab
The correct option is (d).
• Following the provincial elections held in
1937 under the Government of India Act of
1935, the Indian National Congress achieved
a massive electoral victory.
• The Congress formed ministries in seven out
of the eleven provinces, including Bihar,
Madras, and Orissa. It later formed coalition
governments in Assam and the North-West
Frontier Province.
• However, in Punjab, the Congress did not win
a majority. A ministry was formed by the
cross-communal, pro-landlord Unionist Party
under the leadership of Sir Sikandar Hayat
Khan. Bengal was the other major province
where a non-Congress ministry was formed.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q16. Consider the following statements: On the eve
of the launch of Quit India Movement, Mahatma
Gandhi
1. asked the Government servants to resign.
2. asked the soldiers to leave their posts.
3. asked the Princes of the Princely States to accept
the sovereignty of their own people.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (c).
• In his famous "Do or Die" speech on the eve
of the Quit India Movement on August 8,
1942, Mahatma Gandhi gave specific
instructions to different sections of society.
• Statement 1 is incorrect. He did not ask
government servants to resign. Instead, he
asked them to declare their allegiance to the
Congress while continuing their service.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. He did not ask
soldiers to leave their posts. He asked them
to remain in the army but to refuse to fire on
their own compatriots.
• Statement 3 is correct. He specifically
addressed the rulers of the princely states,
asking them to "accept the sovereignty of
their own people" and to align themselves
with the cause of Indian freedom.
• Therefore, only statement 3 is correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q17. What was Komagata Maru?
(a) A political party based in Taiwan
(b) Peasant communist leader of China
(c) A naval ship on voyage to Canada
(d) A Chinese village where Mao Tse Tung began his
Long march
The correct option is (c).
• The Komagata Maru was a Japanese
steamship that became the center of a
famous incident in 1914.
• The ship was chartered by an Indian
businessman, Gurdit Singh, to transport a
group of Indian immigrants (mostly Sikhs
from Punjab) to Canada.
• When the ship arrived in Vancouver,
Canadian authorities, enforcing their
exclusionary immigration laws, refused to
allow the passengers to disembark. After a

two-month standoff, the ship was forced to
return to India, where the passengers were
met with hostility and violence from the
British police. The incident became a rallying
point for Indian nationalists, especially the
Ghadar Party.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q18. Consider the following statements :
1. In the First Round Table Conference Dr. Ambedkar
demanded separate electorates for the depressed
classes.
2. In the Poona Pact, special provisions for
representation of the depressed people in the local
bodies and civil services were made.
3. The Indian National Congress did not take part in
the Third Round Table Conference.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 2 and 3
(c) 1 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct. At the First Round
Table Conference (1930-31), Dr. B.R.
Ambedkar, representing the Depressed
Classes, forcefully argued for their rights and
demanded separate electorates to ensure
their political representation.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Poona Pact
(1932), which was an agreement between Dr.
Ambedkar and Mahatma Gandhi, abandoned
separate electorates but provided for
reserved seats. It also included clauses that
made special provisions for the
representation of the depressed people in
local bodies and for fair recruitment in civil
services.
• Statement 3 is correct. The Indian National
Congress, having withdrawn from the Civil
Disobedience Movement to attend the
Second Round Table Conference, did not take
part in the Third Round Table Conference
(1932). The Congress had resumed its civil

•

disobedience campaign, and the British
government did not invite them.
Since all three statements are correct, the
correct option is (d).
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Q19. Consider the following statements:
1. Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar founded the Bethune
School at Calcutta with the main aim of encouraging
education for women.
2. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay was the first
graduate of the Calcutta University.
3. Keshav Chandra Sen’s campaign against Sati led to
the enactment of a law to ban Sati by the then
Governor General.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 1 and 2
(c) 2 and 3
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is correct. Ishwar Chandra
Vidyasagar was a staunch advocate for
women's education. He was closely
associated with John Elliot Drinkwater
Bethune in founding the Hindu Female School
in Calcutta in 1849, which later came to be
known as Bethune School. Vidyasagar served
as its secretary and was a driving force
behind it.
• Statement 2 is correct. The University of
Calcutta was established in 1857. In 1858,
Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay and Joddu
Nath Bose became the first two graduates of
the university, and indeed of any university in
the British Empire.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. The campaign
against Sati that led to its abolition in 1829
was led by Raja Ram Mohan Roy, not Keshav
Chandra Sen. It was Ram Mohan Roy's
tireless efforts that persuaded GovernorGeneral Lord William Bentinck to enact the
law banning the practice.

•

Therefore, only statements 1 and 2 are
correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

•

Q20. Who among the following was a proponent of
Fabianism as a movement?
(a) Annie Besant
(b) A. O. Hume
(c) Michael Madhusudan Dutt
(d) R. Palme Dutt
The correct option is (a).
• Fabianism is a form of socialism that
advocates for gradual and reformist, rather
than revolutionary, change to achieve a
socialist society. The Fabian Society was
founded in Britain in 1884.
• Annie Besant, before she became deeply
involved in Theosophy and Indian politics,
was a prominent socialist and a leading
member of the Fabian Society in Britain in the
1880s.
• She was a powerful orator and writer who
helped popularize Fabian ideas. Although her
focus later shifted, her early career was
deeply rooted in the Fabian socialist
movement.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

•

Q21. Who among the following repealed the
Vernacular Press Act?
(a) Lord Dufferin
(b) Lord Ripon
(c) Lord Curzon
(d) Lord Hardinge
The correct option is (b).

•

The Vernacular Press Act, a highly repressive
law passed by Lord Lytton in 1878 to censor
the Indian-language press, was widely
condemned by Indian nationalists.
When Lord Ripon became the Viceroy in
1880, he, being a liberal, sought to reverse
many of the reactionary policies of his
predecessor.
In 1882, Lord Ripon repealed the Vernacular
Press Act, an act that was greatly appreciated
by educated Indians and restored freedom to
the Indian press.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2006

Q1. Which portfolio was held by Dr. Rajendra Prasad
in the Interim Government formed in the year 1946?
(a) Defence
(b) External Affairs and Commonwealth Relations
(c) Food and Agriculture
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• The Interim Government of India was formed
on September 2, 1946, to manage the
administration during the transition to
independence.
• In this cabinet, headed by Jawaharlal Nehru
as the Vice President of the Executive
Council, portfolios were distributed among
prominent leaders.
• Dr. Rajendra Prasad, a senior Congress leader
and future President of India, was entrusted
with the crucial portfolio of Food and
Agriculture. This was a particularly
challenging role given the post-war food
shortages in the country.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. Who was the Governor-General of India during
the Sepoy Mutiny?
(a) Lord Canning
(b) Lord Dalhousie
(c) Lord Hardinge
(d) Lord Lytton

The correct option is (a).
• The Sepoy Mutiny, also known as the Revolt
of 1857, was a major turning point in the
history of British India.
• The Governor-General of India during this
tumultuous period was Lord Canning. He had
assumed office in 1856, just a year before the
outbreak of the revolt.
• He oversaw the suppression of the rebellion
and the subsequent transfer of power from
the East India Company to the British Crown
in 1858. After this transfer, his title was
changed, and he became the first Viceroy of
India.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q3. Consider the following statements about Madam
Bhikaji Cama :
1. Madam Cama unfurled the National Flag at the
International Socialist Conference in Paris in the year
1907.
2. Madam Cama served as private secretary to
Dadabhai Naoroji.
3. Madam Cama was born to Parsi parents.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1, 2 and 3
(b) 2 and 3, only
(c) 1 and 2, only
(d) 3 only
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Madam Cama did
unfurl a flag for Indian independence in 1907,
but it was at the International Socialist
Conference in Stuttgart, Germany, not Paris.
This was a historic moment as it was one of

•

•

•

the first times an Indian national flag was
hoisted on foreign soil.
Statement 2 is correct. While in London,
Madam Cama worked closely with Dadabhai
Naoroji and served as his private secretary.
This association deepened her involvement in
the nationalist cause.
Statement 3 is correct. Madam Bhikaji Cama
was born into a wealthy and influential Parsi
family in Bombay (Mumbai).
Therefore, only statements 2 and 3 are
correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. Consider the following statements:
1. The Charter Act 1853 abolished East India
Company’s monopoly of Indian trade.
2. Under the Government of India Act, 1858, the
British Parliament abolished the East India Company
altogether and undertook the responsibility of ruling
India directly.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The East India
Company's monopoly on Indian trade was
abolished by the Charter Act of 1813. The
Charter Act of 1833 further ended its
monopoly on the tea trade and trade with
China. The Charter Act of 1853 merely
renewed the Company's charter without
specifying a time limit.
• Statement 2 is correct. Following the Revolt
of 1857, the British Parliament passed the
Government of India Act, 1858. This
abolished the East India Company and
transferred all its powers, territories, and
revenues directly to the British Crown,
marking the formal beginning of the British
Raj.
• Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q5. In the year 1613, where was the English East
India Company given permission to set up a factory
(trading post)?
(a) Bangalore
(b) Madras
(c) Masulipattam
(d) Surat
The correct option is (d).
• The English East India Company made several
attempts to secure trading rights from the
Mughal Empire in the early 17th century.
• After initial setbacks, Captain Thomas Best
inflicted a naval defeat on the Portuguese off
the coast of Surat in 1612, impressing the
Mughal governor.
• As a result, in 1613, Emperor Jahangir issued
a farman granting the Company permission to
establish a permanent factory at Surat,
becoming the first major English trading
settlement in India.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. With reference to the revolt of the year 1857,
who of the following was betrayed by a friend,
captured and put to death by the British?
(a) Nana Sahib
(b) Kunwar Singh
(c) Khan Bahadur Khan
(d) Tantya Tope
The correct option is (d).
• Tantya Tope (Tatya Tope) was one of the
most brilliant and tenacious military leaders
of the Revolt of 1857, and a close associate of
Nana Sahib.
• After the fall of Gwalior, he waged a
remarkable guerrilla campaign against the
British in the forests of Central India for over
a year.
• In April 1859, while he was asleep in the
Paron forest, he was betrayed by his trusted
friend, Man Singh, the Raja of Narwar. Man
Singh informed the British, leading to his
capture. Tantya Tope was subsequently tried
by a military court and executed by hanging

on April 18, 1859.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q7. Under whose presidency was the Lahore Session
of the Indian National Congress held in the year 1929
wherein a resolution was adopted to gain complete
independence from the British?
(a) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
(b) Gopal Krishna Gokhale
(c) Jawaharlal Nehru
(d) Motilal Nehru
The correct option is (c).
• The Lahore Session of the Indian National
Congress in December 1929 was a landmark
event in the freedom struggle.
• This session was held under the presidency of
Jawaharlal Nehru, representing the rise of the
younger, more radical wing of the party.
• It was at this session that the historic "Poorna
Swaraj" (Complete Independence) resolution
was passed, officially changing the goal of the
Congress from Dominion Status to Complete
Independence. It was also decided to
celebrate January 26, 1930, as the first
Independence Day.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2007
Q1. The song ‘Amar Sonar Bangla’ written during the
Swadeshi Movement of India inspired the liberation
struggle of Bangladesh. Who wrote this song?
(a) Rajni Kanta Sen
(b) Dwijendralal Ray
(c) Mukunda Das
(d) Rabindranath Tagore
The correct option is (d).
• The song "Amar Sonar Bangla" (My Golden
Bengal) was written by the Nobel laureate
Rabindranath Tagore in 1905.
• He wrote it during the Swadeshi Movement
as a protest against the British decision to
partition Bengal along communal lines.
• The song was intended to evoke a spirit of
unity and love for the land of Bengal.

•

•

Decades later, during the Bangladesh
Liberation War of 1971, this song became a
powerful anthem for the Bengali nationalists
fighting for independence from Pakistan.
After its liberation, Bangladesh adopted the
first ten lines of the song as its national
anthem.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

•

•

•

Q2. The first Factory Act restricting the working
hours of women and children, and authorizing local
governments to make necessary rules was adopted
during whose time?
(a) Lord Lytton
(b) Lord Bentinck
(c) Lord Ripon
(d) Lord Canning
The correct option is (c).
• The First Factory Act was passed in 1881
during the viceroyalty of Lord Ripon.
• This was the first piece of labour legislation in
India.
• It was primarily aimed at addressing the poor
working conditions of child laborers in
factories.
• The Act prohibited the employment of
children under the age of 7, limited working
hours for children between 7 and 12, and
mandated safety measures like the fencing of
machinery.
• It also authorized local governments to make
rules for its implementation.
• While it also had provisions for women, its
main focus was on child labor.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Which one of the following aroused a wave of
popular indignation that led to the massacre by the
British at Jallianwala Bagh?
(a) The Arms Act
(b) The Public Safety Act
(c) The Rowlatt Act
(d) The Vernacular Press Act
The correct option is (c).

•
•

In March 1919, the British government passed
the Anarchical and Revolutionary Crimes Act,
which came to be popularly known as The
Rowlatt Act.
This act gave the government draconian
powers to suppress political activities,
including the power to arrest and imprison
any person without trial for up to two years.
The act was seen as a betrayal of wartime
promises of reform and aroused a massive
wave of popular indignation across India.
Mahatma Gandhi called for a nationwide
satyagraha against this "Black Act."
It was in the context of these widespread
protests that the tragic massacre at
Jallianwala Bagh took place in Amritsar on
April 13, 1919.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. At which one of the following places did
Mahatma Gandhi first start his Satyagraha in India?
(a) Ahmedabad
(b) Bardoli
(c) Champaran
(d) Kheda
The correct option is (c).
• After returning to India from South Africa,
Mahatma Gandhi's first major experiment
with Satyagraha was in Champaran, Bihar, in
1917.
• He went there at the request of a local
peasant, Rajkumar Shukla, to investigate and
lead a struggle against the oppressive
tinkathia system, which forced farmers to
cultivate indigo for European planters.
• His successful campaign in Champaran,
involving non-violent civil disobedience and a
detailed inquiry into the peasants' grievances,
established his leadership in India and
demonstrated the efficacy of Satyagraha.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q5. Who among the following started the newspaper
Shome Prakash?
(a) Dayanand Saraswati

(b) Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
(c) Raja Rammohan Roy
(d) Surendranath Banerjee
The correct option is (b).
• The Bengali weekly newspaper "Somprakash"
was started in 1858.
• While it was edited and published by
Dwarkanath Vidyabhusan, the inspiration
and driving force behind its establishment
was Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar.
• Vidyasagar encouraged the publication to
propagate progressive and reformist ideas.
• "Somprakash" became a prominent voice in
19th-century Bengal, known for its fearless
criticism of social evils and colonial policies,
particularly the oppression of indigo planters.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. The ruler of which one of the following States
was removed from power by the British on the
pretext of misgovernance?
(a) Awadh
(b) Jhansi
(c) Nagpur
(d) Satara
The correct option is (a).
• In 1856, Lord Dalhousie annexed the kingdom
of Awadh (Oudh).
• Unlike other annexations under the Doctrine
of Lapse, the annexation of Awadh was
justified on the pretext of misgovernance.
• The British claimed that Nawab Wajid Ali
Shah was inept and that his administration
was corrupt and oppressive, leading to the
suffering of his subjects.
• The Nawab was deposed and exiled to
Calcutta.
• This annexation caused widespread
resentment and was a major contributing
factor to the Revolt of 1857.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q7. Who among the following Europeans were the
last to come to pre-independence India as traders?
(a) Dutch

(b) English
(c) French
(d) Portuguese
The correct option is (c).
• To arrange the arrival of European powers in
chronological order:
o Portuguese: Vasco da Gama reached
Calicut in 1498.
o Dutch: Formed in 1602; first factory at
Masulipatnam in 1605.
o English: Formed in 1600; first factory
at Surat in 1613.
o French: Formed in 1664; first factory
at Surat in 1668.
• Therefore, the French were the last major
European power to establish trading
presence.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Consider the following statements:
1. Robert Clive was the first Governor-General of
Bengal.
2. William Bentinck was the first GovernorGeneral of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. Robert Clive was the
Governor of Bengal, but the first GovernorGeneral of Bengal was Warren Hastings
under the Regulating Act of 1773.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Charter Act of
1833 changed the designation to GovernorGeneral of India, and Lord William Bentinck
became the first to hold this title.
• Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q9. Which one of the following was the first fort
constructed by the British in India?
(a) Fort William
(b) Fort St. George

(c) Fort St. David
(d) Fort St. Angelo
The correct option is (b).
• The first major fortress constructed by the
British was Fort St. George.
• Construction began in 1644 at Madras
(Chennai).
• The fort became the nucleus of the city and
the headquarters of the Madras Presidency.
• Fort William was built later in Calcutta; Fort
St. Angelo was Portuguese; Fort St. David was
also later.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q10. Consider the following statements :
1. Jawaharlal Nehru was in his fourth term as the
Prime Minister of India at the time of his
death.
2. Jawaharlal Nehru represented the Rae Bareilly
constituency as a Member of Parliament.
3. The first non-Congress Prime Minister of India
assumed the Office in the year 1977.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 and 2
(b) 3 only
(c) 1 only
(d) 1 and 3
The correct option is (d).
• Statement 1 is correct. Nehru became PM in
1947, won 1952, 1957, 1962 elections, and
died in his fourth term (1964).
• Statement 2 is incorrect. He represented
Phulpur, not Rae Bareilly.
• Statement 3 is correct. Morarji Desai, of
Janata Party, became the first non-Congress
PM in 1977.
• Therefore, statements 1 and 3 are correct.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2008
Q1. Where was the First Session of the Indian
National Congress held in December 1885?
(a) Ahmedabad
(b) Bombay

(c) Calcutta
(d) Delhi
The correct option is (b).
• The Indian National Congress (INC) was
founded in 1885 by Allan Octavian Hume,
along with prominent Indian leaders.
• The first session of the INC was held
December 28–31, 1885, in Bombay
(Mumbai).
• The venue was the hall of Gokuldas Tejpal
Sanskrit College. The session was presided
over by Womesh Chandra Bonnerjee and was
attended by 72 delegates from various parts
of India, marking the beginning of an
organized, all-India nationalist movement.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q2. Assertion (A): The Congress in all the provinces
resigned in the year 1939. Reason (R): The Congress
did not accept the decision of the Viceroy to declare
war against Germany in the context of the Second
World War.
(a) Both A and R are true, and R is the correct
explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is not a correct
explanation of A
(c) A is true, but R is false
(d) A is false, but R is true
The correct option is (a).
• Assertion (A) is true. In October–November
1939, the Congress ministries in the seven
provinces where they were in power resigned
en masse.
• Reason (R) is also true. The Viceroy, Lord
Linlithgow, had declared India to be at war
with Germany without consulting any Indian
leaders or the elected provincial
governments. The Congress demanded a
clear statement of Britain’s war aims and the
promise of independence after the war,
which the British government refused.
• The reason directly and correctly explains the
assertion. The unilateral decision to drag
India into the war was seen as a grave insult
to Indian self-respect and led to the Congress

ministries resigning in protest.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
•

Q3. Which one of the following was a journal
brought out by Abul Kalam Azad?
(a) Al-Hilal
(b) Comrade
(c) The Indian Sociologist
(d) Zamindar
The correct option is (a).
• Maulana Abul Kalam Azad, a prominent
leader of the Indian independence
movement, was also a brilliant scholar and
journalist.
• In 1912, he started a weekly Urdu newspaper
called Al-Hilal from Calcutta.
• Through this journal, he espoused a message
of Indian nationalism, Hindu–Muslim unity,
and anti-imperialism, using a powerful and
revolutionary new style of Urdu prose. The
paper became immensely popular but was
banned by the British government in 1914
under the Press Act.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. Who among the following Gandhian followers
was a teacher by profession?
(a) A.N. Sinha
(b) Braj Kishore Prasad
(c) J.B. Kripalani
(d) Rajendra Prasad
The correct option is (c).
• J.B. Kripalani, popularly known as Acharya
Kripalani, was a prominent Gandhian leader

•

and a key figure in the Indian National
Congress.
Before dedicating his life to the freedom
struggle, he had a distinguished career as an
educationist. He worked as a teacher and
professor at various institutions, including the
Banaras Hindu University.
His title "Acharya" (teacher) reflects his
background and his role as a respected
intellectual and teacher within the nationalist
movement.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q5. Who among the following rejected the title of
knighthood and refused to accept a position in the
Council of the Secretary of State for India?
(a) Motilal Nehru
(b) M.G. Ranade
(c) G.K. Gokhale
(d) B.G. Tilak
The correct option is (c).
• Gopal Krishna Gokhale, the eminent
Moderate leader and political mentor of
Mahatma Gandhi, was highly respected by
the British for his intellect and
statesmanship.
• The British government offered him the title
of Knighthood, but Gokhale politely rejected
this honor, believing that accepting such a
title would compromise his position as a
nationalist leader.
• He also refused a position in the Council of
the Secretary of State for India for similar
reasons.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q6. During the Indian Freedom Struggle, who of the
following raised an army called ‘Free Indian Legion’?
(a) Lala Hardayal
(b) Rashbehari Bose
(c) Subhas Chandra Bose
(d) V.D. Savarkar
The correct option is (c).

•

•

•

The Free Indian Legion (Indische Legion,
Infantry Regiment 950) was a military unit
raised during World War II in Nazi Germany.
The formation of this legion was initiated by
Subhas Chandra Bose during his time in Berlin
in 1941.
The legion was composed of Indian prisoners
of war captured by the Axis powers in North
Africa and Indian expatriates in Europe,
intended to fight against the British for
India’s independence with German
assistance.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q7. Which one of the following suggested the
reconstitution of the Viceroy’s Executive Council in
which all the portfolios including that of War
Members were to be held by the Indian leaders?
(a) Simon Commission
(b) Shimla Conference
(c) Cripps Proposal
(d) Cabinet Mission
The correct option is (b).
• The Shimla Conference was convened by
Viceroy Lord Wavell in June–July 1945 to
discuss the Wavell Plan.
• A key feature of the Wavell Plan was the
reconstitution of the Viceroy’s Executive
Council to function as an interim
government.
• The plan proposed that all portfolios,
including the crucial War Member, would be
held by Indian leaders, with the exception of
the Viceroy and the Commander-in-Chief; the
conference failed due to a deadlock between
the Congress and the Muslim League over
representation.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q8. Who among the following used the phrase ‘UnBritish’ to criticize the English colonial control of
India?
(a) Anandmohan Bose
(b) Badruddin Tyabji

(c) Dadabhai Naoroji
(d) Pherozeshah Mehta
The correct option is (c).
• Dadabhai Naoroji, the "Grand Old Man of
India," developed a powerful economic
critique of British rule.
• He famously used the term "Un-British" to
describe the nature of British colonial control
in India; the title of his seminal work was
Poverty and Un-British Rule in India.
• He contrasted the "British" principles of
justice and freedom with the "Un-British"
reality of colonial exploitation.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q9. Who among the following gave a systematic
critique of the moderate politics of the Indian
National Congress in a series of articles entitled New
Lamps for Old?
(a) Aurobindo Ghosh
(b) R.C. Dutt
(c) Syed Ahmad Khan
(d) Viraraghavachari
The correct option is (a).
• In 1893–94, Aurobindo Ghosh wrote a series
of articles for Indu Prakash titled "New Lamps
for Old."
• He launched a sharp and systematic critique
of the "pray and petition" politics of the
Moderate-led Indian National Congress.
• He called for a more radical, self-reliant, and
mass-based approach to the freedom
struggle, an early and articulate expression of
Extremist ideology.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q10. Who was the Viceroy of India when the Rowlatt
Act was Passed?
(a) Lord Irwin
(b) Lord Reading
(c) Lord Chelmsford
(d) Lord Wavell
The correct option is (c).
• The Rowlatt Act (officially the Anarchical and
Revolutionary Crimes Act) was passed in
March 1919.
• The Viceroy of India during this period was
Lord Chelmsford (1916–1921).
• The act was passed during his tenure, and the
subsequent nationwide protests, including
the Jallianwala Bagh massacre, also occurred
while he was the Viceroy.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2009
Q1. Which one of the following began with the Dandi
March?
(a) Home Rule Movement
(b) Non-Cooperation Movement
(c) Civil Disobedience Movement
(d) Quit India Movement
The correct option is (c).
• The Dandi March, also known as the Salt
March, was a pivotal event in the Indian
freedom struggle led by Mahatma Gandhi.
• It began on March 12, 1930, from Sabarmati
Ashram and concluded on April 6, 1930, when
Gandhi broke the British salt law at the
coastal village of Dandi.
• This act of defiance marked the formal
inauguration and beginning of the
nationwide Civil Disobedience Movement,
which involved the widespread and nonviolent breaking of various colonial laws.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q2. With which one of the following movements is
the slogan ‘’Do or Die” associated?
(a) Swadeshi Movement
(b) Non-Cooperation Movement

(c) Civil Disobedience Movement
(d) Quit India Movement
The correct option is (d).
• The powerful slogan "Do or Die" (Karo ya
Maro) was given by Mahatma Gandhi on the
eve of the Quit India Movement.
• In his speech at the Gowalia Tank Maidan in
Bombay on August 8, 1942, after the All-India
Congress Committee passed the Quit India
resolution, Gandhi called for a final and
decisive struggle.
• He urged the Indian people to act as free
citizens and to either free India or die in the
attempt, signaling a more intense and
determined phase of the freedom struggle.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q3. In the context of the Indian freedom struggle,
16th October 1905 is well known for which one of
the following reasons?
(a) The formal proclamation of Swadeshi Movement
was made in Calcutta town hall
(b) Partition of Bengal took effect
(c) Dadabhai Naoroji declared that the goal of Indian
National Congress was Swaraj
(d) Lokmanya Tilak started Swadeshi Movement in
Poona
The correct option is (b).
• October 16, 1905, is a significant date
because on this day, the British government's
decision to partition Bengal took effect.
• The province was divided into “Bengal”
(western Bengal + Bihar & Orissa) and
“Eastern Bengal and Assam.”
• This act was widely seen as a “divide and
rule” tactic to weaken the nerve center of
Indian nationalism.
• The day was observed as a day of mourning
throughout Bengal, marked by hartals,
fasting, and processions, and it gave a
massive impetus to the Swadeshi and
Boycott movements.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q4. In the “Individual Satyagraha”, Vinoba Bhave
was chosen as the first Satyagrahi. Who was the
second?
(a) Dr. Rajendra Prasad
(b) Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru
(c) C. Rajagopalachari
(d) Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
The correct option is (b).
• The Individual Satyagraha was launched by
Mahatma Gandhi in October 1940 as a
limited, non-violent protest against India's
forced participation in World War II.
• It involved selected individuals courting
arrest by making anti-war speeches.
• The first Satyagrahi was Acharya Vinoba
Bhave.
• After his arrest, the second Satyagrahi was
Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru, followed by Brahma
Datt as the third.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Consider the following statements: The Cripps
Proposals include the provision for
1. Full independence for India
2. Creation of a Constitution-making body
Which of the given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect. The Cripps proposals
did not offer full independence; they offered
Dominion Status after the war.
• Statement 2 is correct. A key provision was
the creation of a Constitution-making body
after the war. This Constituent Assembly
would be elected by provincial assemblies
and nominated by princely states.
• Therefore, only statement 2 is correct.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. During the freedom struggle, Aruna Asaf Ali was
a major woman organizer of underground activity in:
(a) Civil Disobedience Movement

(b) Non-Cooperation Movement
(c) Quit India Movement
(d) Swadeshi Movement
The correct option is (c).
• Aruna Asaf Ali is one of the most heroic
figures of the Indian freedom struggle,
particularly for her role in the Quit India
Movement (1942).
• After the arrest of top Congress leaders on
August 9, 1942, she defied the British by
hoisting the Congress flag at Gowalia Tank
Maidan, marking the start of the movement.
• She then went underground and became a
major organizer of clandestine revolutionary
activities, evading arrest for several years.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Who of the following is the author of a collection
of poems called “Golden Threshold”?
(a) Aruna Asaf Ali
(b) Annie Besant
(c) Sarojini Naidu
(d) Vijayalakshmi Pandit
The correct option is (c).
• Sarojini Naidu, the “Nightingale of India,”
was not only a prominent freedom fighter and
the first Indian woman President of the INC,
but also a celebrated poet.
• Her first collection of poems, published in
1905, was titled "The Golden Threshold".
• This collection, along with “The Bird of Time”
and “The Broken Wing”, established her as a
major Indo-Anglian poet.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q8. With reference to the Union Government,
consider the following statements :
1. The number of Ministries at the Centre on
15th August 1947 was 18.
2. The number of Ministries at the Centre at
present is 36.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. On 15th August 1947,
the first Cabinet under Jawaharlal Nehru had
18 ministries.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The number of
ministries is not fixed; it changes with each
government. It was not 36 at the time of the
question.
• Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q9. In collaboration with David Hare and Alexander
Duff, who of the following established Hindu College
at Calcutta?
(a) Henry Louis Vivian Derozio
(b) Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
(c) Keshab Chandra Sen
(d) Raja Rammohan Roy
The correct option is (d).
• Hindu College (later Presidency University)
was founded in 1817 in Calcutta.
• It was established by David Hare, with active
support from Raja Rammohan Roy and
others.
• It was a pioneering institution for modern
Western education in India.
• Alexander Duff also promoted Western
education but played a less direct role in its
founding.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q10. During the Indian Freedom Struggle, why did
the Rowlatt Act arouse popular indignation?
(a) It curtailed the freedom of religion

(b) It suppressed the Indian traditional education
(c) It authorized the government to imprison people
without trial
(d) It curbed the trade union activities
The correct option is (c).
• The Rowlatt Act (1919) gave the British
government arbitrary powers, including
imprisonment without trial for up to two
years.
• It was a suspension of habeas corpus and was
seen as tyrannical and a betrayal of post-war
promises of reform.
• This led to widespread anger and protest
across India.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q11. Who of the following Prime Ministers sent the
Cripps Mission to India?
(a) James Ramsay MacDonald
(b) Stanley Baldwin
(c) Neville Chamberlain
(d) Winston Churchill
The correct option is (d).
• The Cripps Mission was sent in March 1942,
during World War II.
• It was dispatched by the British War Cabinet
headed by Winston Churchill.
• Churchill’s government sent Sir Stafford
Cripps to negotiate with Indian leaders and
secure their cooperation in the war effort.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q12. Consider the following statements:
1. The discussions in the Third Round Table
Conference eventually led to the passing of
the Government of India Act of 1935.
2. The Government of India Act of 1935
provided for the establishment of an All India
Federation to be based on a Union of the
provinces of British India and the Princely
States.
Which of the above statements is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2

(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct. The deliberations of
the three Round Table Conferences and the
Simon Commission Report led to a White
Paper, which resulted in the Government of
India Act, 1935.
• Statement 2 is correct. The Act provided for
an All-India Federation, consisting of British
Indian provinces and princely states that
joined voluntarily. (The federation, however,
was never implemented).
• Since both statements are correct, the correct
option is (c).
Hence, option (c) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2010
Q1. For the Karachi session of Indian National
Congress in 1931, presided over by Sardar Patel, who
drafted the Resolution on Fundamental Rights and
Economic Programme?
(a) Mahatma Gandhi
(b) Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru
(c) Dr. Rajendra Prasad
(d) Dr. B. R. Ambedkar
The correct option is (b).
• The Karachi session of the Indian National
Congress in 1931 is a landmark for its
resolution on Fundamental Rights and
National Economic Programme.
• This resolution outlined the Congress's vision
for a free India, including civil liberties and a
socio-economic agenda with a socialist
orientation.
• The resolution was primarily drafted by
Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru, reflecting his
socialist ideals. While it was a collective
effort and may have been modified to
achieve consensus, Nehru was the principal
author of the initial draft.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q2. With reference to the Simon Commission’s
recommendations, which one of the following
statements is correct?
(a) It recommended the replacement of diarchy with
responsible government in the provinces.
(b) It proposed the setting up of an inter-provincial
council under the Home Department.
(c) It suggested the abolition of the bicameral
legislature at the Centre.
(d) It recommended the creation of an Indian Police
Service, with a provision for increased pay and
allowances for British recruits as compared to Indian
recruits.
The correct option is (a).
• The Simon Commission was appointed in
1927 to review the functioning of the
Government of India Act, 1919. Its report,
published in 1930, made several key
recommendations.
• The most significant of these was that the
system of diarchy (dual government) in the
provinces was unworkable and should be
abolished.
• In its place, the commission recommended
the introduction of a full responsible
government in the provinces, where ministers
would be responsible to the provincial
legislatures for all departments.
• This recommendation was a major step
towards provincial autonomy, which was
later incorporated into the Government of
India Act, 1935.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q3. Who among the following were official Congress
negotiators with the Cripps Mission?
(a) Mahatma Gandhi and Sardar Patel
(b) Acharya J. B. Kripalani and C. Rajagopalachari
(c) Pandit Nehru and Maulana Azad
(d) Dr. Rajendra Prasad and Rafi Ahmed Kidwai
The correct option is (c).
• The Cripps Mission arrived in India in March
1942 to negotiate with Indian leaders and
secure their support for the British war effort.

•

•

•

The Indian National Congress appointed its
official negotiators to hold discussions with
Sir Stafford Cripps.
The two leaders entrusted with this
responsibility were Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru
and the then Congress President, Maulana
Abul Kalam Azad.
They held extensive discussions with Cripps,
but the talks ultimately failed as the Congress
rejected the proposals.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q4. Four resolutions were passed at the famous
Calcutta session of the Indian National Congress in
1906. The question of either retention OR of
rejection of these four resolutions became the cause
of a split in Congress at the next Congress session
held in Surat in 1907. Which one of the following was
not one of those resolutions?
(a) Annulment of partition of Bengal
(b) Boycott
(c) National education
(d) Swadeshi
The correct option is (a).
• The Calcutta session of the Congress in 1906,
presided over by Dadabhai Naoroji, was a
compromise between the Moderates and the
Extremists.
• Under pressure from the Extremists, four key
resolutions were passed. These were on
Swaraj (self-government), Swadeshi
(promotion of indigenous goods), Boycott (of
foreign goods), and National Education.
• While the annulment of the partition of
Bengal was the central demand of the
ongoing movement, it was not one of the
four specific programmatic resolutions that
became the point of contention leading to the
Surat Split in 1907.
• The dispute was over the interpretation and
scope of the resolutions on Swaraj, Swadeshi,
Boycott, and National Education.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q5. After the Quit India Movement, C.
Rajagopalachari issued a pamphlet entitled ‘The Way
Out”. Which one of the following was a proposal in
this pamphlet?
(a) The establishment of a “War Advisory Council”
composed of representatives of British India and the
Indian States
(b) Reconstitution of the Central Executive Council in
such a way that all its members, except the Governor
General and the Commander-in-Chief should be
Indian leaders
(c) Fresh elections to the Central and Provincial
Legislatures to be held at the end of 1945 and the
Constitution making body to be convened as soon as
possible
(d) A solution for the constitutional deadlock
The correct option is (d).
• In 1944, with the nationalist leadership in jail
and a political stalemate prevailing, C.
Rajagopalachari (Rajaji) put forward a
formula to break the impasse. This was
published in a pamphlet titled "The Way
Out."
• The core of the pamphlet was a solution for
the constitutional deadlock between the
Congress and the Muslim League.
• It proposed that the Muslim League should
cooperate with the Congress in forming a
provisional interim government for the
transitional period.
• After the war, a commission would be
appointed to demarcate contiguous districts
in the north-west and north-east where
Muslims were in an absolute majority.
• A plebiscite of all inhabitants in these areas
would then decide whether or not to form a
separate sovereign state. This was essentially
a tacit acceptance of the principle of Pakistan
to achieve a settlement.
Hence, option (d) is correct.
Q6. Who among the following Governor Generals
created the Covenanted Civil Service of India which
later came to be known as the Indian Civil Service?
(a) Warren Hastings

(b) Wellesley
(c) Cornwallis
(d) William Bentinck
The correct option is (c).
• Lord Cornwallis, who served as GovernorGeneral from 1786 to 1793, is widely
regarded as the "Father of the Indian Civil
Service."
• He undertook a major overhaul of the
Company's administration to root out
corruption and improve efficiency.
• He separated the commercial and
administrative functions of the Company's
employees and created a professional cadre
of Company servants who were to be paid
handsome salaries but were forbidden from
engaging in private trade.
• This organized and regulated service came to
be known as the Covenanted Civil Service,
which formed the basis for what later became
the elite Indian Civil Service (ICS).
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q7. What was the immediate cause for the launch of
the Swadeshi movement?
(a) The partition of Bengal done by Lord Curzon
(b) A sentence of 18 months rigorous imprisonment
imposed on Lokmanya Tilak
(c) The arrest and deportation of Lala Lajpat Rai and
Ajit Singh; and passing of the Punjab Colonization Bill
(d) Death sentence pronounced on the Chapekar

brothers
The correct option is (a).
• The Swadeshi Movement was a direct and
spontaneous reaction to the British
government's decision to partition the
province of Bengal.
• In July 1905, the Viceroy, Lord Curzon,
announced the partition of Bengal, ostensibly
for administrative reasons but widely seen as
a political move to "divide and rule" by
weakening the nerve center of Indian
nationalism.
• This act caused widespread outrage and led
to the launch of the Swadeshi Movement,
which involved the boycott of British goods
and the promotion of indigenous products as
a form of protest.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q8. By a regulation in 1793, the District Collector was
deprived of his judicial powers and made the
collecting agent only. What was the reason for such
regulation?
(a) Lord Cornwallis felt that the District Collector’s
efficiency of revenue collection would enormously
increase without the burden of other work
(b) Lord Cornwallis felt that Judicial power should
compulsorily be in the hands of Europeans while
Indians can be given the job of revenue collection in
the districts
(c) Lord Cornwallis was alarmed at the extent of
power concentrated in the District Collector and felt
that such absolute power was undesirable in one
person
(d) The judicial work demanded a deep knowledge of
India and a good training in law and Lord Cornwallis
felt that District Collector should be only a revenue
collector
The correct option is (c).
• As part of his administrative reforms, Lord
Cornwallis introduced the principle of
separation of powers.
• Initially, the District Collector held vast
powers, combining the roles of revenue
collector, judge, and magistrate.

•
•

•

Lord Cornwallis was alarmed at this immense
concentration of power in a single official.
He believed that combining revenue and
judicial functions in one person created a
system prone to corruption and oppression,
as the same person who assessed revenue
could also adjudicate disputes related to it.
Therefore, based on the principle that such
absolute power was undesirable in one
person, the Cornwallis Code of 1793
separated the judiciary from the executive,
depriving the Collector of his judicial powers
and confining him to revenue collection.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q9. Consider the following statements:
1. The “Bombay Manifesto” signed in 1936
openly opposed the preaching of socialist
ideals.
2. It evoked support from a large section of the
business community from all across India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. In 1936, Jawaharlal
Nehru, in his presidential address at the
Lucknow session of the Congress, strongly
advocated for socialism. This alarmed the
conservative, right-wing business interests.
• In response, 21 leading businessmen of
Bombay issued a public statement, known as
the "Bombay Manifesto", which openly
opposed the preaching of socialist ideals,
arguing that they were detrimental to private
property and national prosperity.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The manifesto did
not evoke widespread support from the
business community across India. In fact,
other prominent capitalists, like G.D. Birla,
criticized the manifesto as a tactical blunder
that would only create a public confrontation
with the nationalist leadership.

•

•

The support for the manifesto was largely
confined to the group of Bombay
businessmen who signed it.
Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q10. With reference to Pondicherry (now
Puducherry), consider the following statements :
1. The first European power to occupy
Pondicherry was the Portuguese.
2. The second European power to occupy
Pondicherry were the French.
3. The English never occupied Pondicherry.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 and 3 only
(c) 3 only
(d) 1, 2 and 3
The correct option is (a).
• Statement 1 is correct. The Portuguese were
indeed the first European power to establish
a trading post in the region of Pondicherry in
the 16th century.
• Statement 2 is incorrect. The French were
not the second power. After the Portuguese,
the Dutch established a presence in the area
before the French East India Company set up
its main settlement there in 1674.
• Statement 3 is incorrect. The English (British)
did occupy Pondicherry on several occasions
during their wars with the French in the 18th
century. For instance, they captured it in 1761
during the Third Carnatic War and held it for
periods before it was returned to the French
through treaties.
• Therefore, only statement 1 is correct.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
UPSC PYQs - 2011
Q1. With reference to Indian freedom struggle, Usha
Mehta is well-known for?
(a) Running the secret Congress radio in the wake of
the Quit India movement.
(b) Participating in the second round table

conference.
(c) Leading a contingent of the Indian national army.
(d) Assisting in the formation of the Interim
government under Pandit Jawaharlal Nehru.
The correct option is (a).
• Usha Mehta was a dedicated Gandhian and
freedom fighter who played a uniquely heroic
role during the Quit India Movement of 1942.
• After the British government arrested all the
top Congress leaders on August 9, 1942, the
movement went underground.
• Usha Mehta, along with her associates,
established and operated a secret
underground radio station, known as the
Congress Radio.
• From this secret location, she broadcasted
uncensored news, patriotic messages, and
speeches from nationalist leaders, keeping
the spirit of the movement alive and
countering British propaganda.
• Her bravery in running this clandestine radio
made her a celebrated figure of the Quit India
Movement.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. Who among the following was the President of
the Indian National Congress at the time of
independence?
(a) Mahatma Gandhi
(b) Jawaharlal Nehru
(c) J.B. Kripalani
(d) Sardar Patel
The correct option is (c).
• The transfer of power and India's
independence occurred in August 1947.
• The President of the Indian National
Congress during this momentous and
turbulent period was Acharya J.B. Kripalani.
• He was elected as the Congress President for
the Meerut session in November 1946 and
held the post throughout the final
negotiations, the partition, and the dawn of
independence.

•

He resigned from the presidency in
November 1947, shortly after independence.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q3. With reference to the period of Indian freedom
struggle, which of the following was/were
recommended by the Nehru report?
1. Complete independence for India.
2. Joint electorates for reservation of seats for
minorities.
3. Provision of fundamental rights for the people
of India in the constitution.
Select the correct answer using the codes given
below:
(a) 1 only.
(b) 2 and 3 only.
(c) 1 and 3 only.
(d) 1, 2, and 3.
The correct option is (b).
• The Nehru Report of 1928 was a
constitutional draft prepared by a committee
