Q2. Consider the following statements:
1. The Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms of 1919
recommended granting voting rights to all
the women above the age of 21.
2. The Government of India Act of 1935 gave
women reserved seats in the legislature.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Montagu-Chelmsford Act, 1919:
o By demarcating and separating the
central and provincial subjects, it
relaxed the central control over the
provinces.
o Divided the provincial subjects into
Transferred and Reserved subjects:
This dual scheme of governance was
known as ‘dyarchy’.
o Introduced bicameralism and direct
elections in the country.
o It required 3 out of 6 members in the
Viceroy’s Executive Council to be
Indian.
o Recommended the voting rights to
women in limited numbers to be
extended on the basis of property,
tax or education.
o Extended the principle of communal
representation among the Sikhs,
Indian Christians, Anglo-Indians and
Europeans.
• Government of India Act of 1935 Features:
o Act divided the powers between the
Centre and units in terms of three
lists—Federal List, Provincial List and
the Concurrent List. Residuary
powers were given to the Viceroy.
o It abolished dyarchy in the provinces
and introduced ‘provincial autonomy’
in its place.
o It introduced bicameralism in six out
of eleven provinces.
o It extended the principle of separate
electorates for depressed classes
(scheduled castes), women and
labour (workers).

o

o

Extended franchise: About 10 per
cent of the total population got the
voting right.
It provided for the establishment of a
Reserve Bank of India and Public
Service Commissions.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q3. With reference to 8th August 1942 in Indian
history, which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) The Quit India Resolution was adopted by the
AICC.
(b) The Viceroy’s Executive Council was expanded to
include more Indians.
(c) The Congress ministries resigned in seven
provinces.
(d) Cripps proposed an Indian Union with full
Dominion Status once the Second World War was
over.
The correct option is (a).
• Option (a) is correct: Quit India Resolution
was ratified in a Congress meeting at Gowalia
Tank on 8th August 1942. Apart from QIM,
other resolutions involved the Civil
Disobedience Movement against British Rule.
The mantra of “Do or Die” was adopted in this
Session by Gandhiji.
• Option (b) is incorrect: More Indians were
added to Viceroy’s executive council by the
Government of India Act 1919. First time, an
Indian was added in this council by the
Government of India Act 1909.
• Option (c) is incorrect: The Congress
Ministries resigned from seven provinces in
1939 in protest because the then Viceroy Lord
Linlithgow’s action declared India to be
belligerent in WW-II without consulting
Indians.
• Option (d) is incorrect: Though Cripps
proposed dominion status to India and failure
of the Cripps Mission resulted in a nationwide
Quit India Movement, this was not related
specifically to 8 August 1942.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q4. In the context of Colonial India, Shah Nawaz
Khan, Prem Kumar Sehgal and Gurbaksh Singh
Dhillon are remembered as:
(a) leaders of Swadeshi and Boycott Movement
(b) members of the Interim Government in 1946
(c) members of the Drafting Committee in the
Constituent Assembly
(d) officers of the Indian National Army
The correct option is (d).
• Indian National Army (INA) Trials/ Red Fort
Trials:
o Between November 1945 and May
1946, the INA officers were brought
before a court martial at the Red Fort
in Delhi. About ten court-martials
were held in public.
o By holding open trials in the Red Fort,
Claude Auchinleck, the commanderin-chief of the British-Indian army,
hoped to sway public opinion against
the INA.
o Major General Shah Nawaz Khan,
Colonel Prem Kumar Sahgal and
Colonel Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon were
three of the senior-most officers of
INA and trusted colleagues of Netaji.
o After considerable demonstrations
and unrest in India, they were forced
to be released after the British courtmartialed them at Red Fort in 1945
and sentenced them to death (later
commuted/released).
o Jawaharlal Nehru and others were
involved in their legal defence.
Hence, option (d) is correct.

Secretary with Hindu Female School which later
came to be known as Bethune Female School?
(a) Annie Besant
(b) Debendranath Tagore
(c) Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
(d) Sarojini Naidu
The correct option is (c).

•

•

Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar (1820-1891) was
appointed as the secretary of Bethune
School/College established in 1849.
Bethune School began as Hindu Female
School in 1849 and was renamed Bethune
School in 1856. In 1856, the Government
took charge of the Hindu Female School, later
renamed Bethune School.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

Q6. With reference to Madanapalle of Andhra
Pradesh, which one of the following statements is
correct?
(a) Pingali Venkayya designed the tricolour Indian
National Flag here.
(b) Pattabhi Sitaramaiah led the Quit India Movement
of Andhra region from here.
(c) Rabindranath Tagore translated the National
Anthem from Bengali to English here.
(d) Madame Blavatsky and Colonel Olcott set up
headquarters of Theosophical Society first here.
The correct option is (c).
• Option (a) is incorrect: The Indian tricolour
was designed by Pingali Venkayya, who
presented it in 1921 during the AICC session in
Vijayawada, not Madanapalle.
• Option (b) is incorrect: During QIM in Andhra
the Kurnool Circular was drafted by Kala
Venkat Rao and sent via Dr. Pattabhi
Sitaramaiah; this does not equate to leading
QIM from Madanapalle.
• Option (c) is correct: Rabindranath Tagore
translated the National Anthem from Bengali
to English at Madanapalle.
• Option (d) is incorrect: The Theosophical
Society was founded in New York in 1875 and

its Indian headquarters were later established
in Adyar, Madras (now Chennai), not
Madanapalle.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q7. Who among the following is associated with
‘Song from Prison’, a translation of ancient Indian
religious lyrics in English?
(a) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
(b) Jawaharlal Nehru
(c) Mohandas Karamchand Gandhi
(d) Sarojini Naidu
The correct option is (c).

1942; Gandhi was arrested on 9 August 1942.
He undertook a 21-day fast in February 1943
to protest Government actions.
• Mahatma Gandhi wrote “Songs of Prison”
during his imprisonment in Yerwada Jail,
Poona.
• He translated hymns from the Upanishads
and poems by Indian saint poets into English
and that collection was published as Songs
from the prison.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2023

Q1. With reference to Indian History, Alexander Rea,
A. H. Longhurst, Robert Sewell, James Burgess and
Walter Elliot were associated with
(a) archaeological excavations
(b) establishment of English Press in Colonial India
(c) establishment of Churches in Princely States
(d) construction of railways in Colonial India
The correct option is (a).
• Alexander Rea (1858–1924) was an
archaeologist who worked mainly in South
British India and is known for unearthing a
sarcophagus from the hillocks of Pallavaram
in Tamil Nadu.
o He arrived in India in 1882 and
became an assistant of Burgess in the
Archaeological Survey of India
(A.S.I.), Madras Division.
o Later, he became Superintendent of
the Archaeological Survey of
Southern India (1902).
• Albert Henry Longhurst (1876–1955) was an
archaeologist and art historian who worked
in India and Ceylon (Sri Lanka).
o He was in charge of the systematic
excavations at Nagarjunakonda
(1927–1931).
• Robert Sewell (1845–1925) was a civil servant
in the Madras Presidency in colonial India.
o He undertook archaeological work at
the Amaravati stupa, previously

surveyed by Colin Mackenzie and
Walter Elliot (1803–1887).
• James Burgess (1832–1916) was the founder
of the Indian Antiquary (1872) and an
important archaeologist of British India in the
19th century.
o He served as the Director General of
the Archaeological Survey of India
(1886–1889).
• Walter Elliot (1803–1887) was among the
earliest British officials to undertake
archaeological surveys and inscriptions
studies in South India, contributing to the
understanding of ancient Indian history and
monuments.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. Consider the following statements:
Statement-I: 7th August is declared as the National
Handloom Day.
Statement-II: It was in 1905 that the Swadeshi
Movement was launched on the same day.
Which one of the following is correct in respect of
the above statements?
(a) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is the correct explanation for Statement-I
(b) Both Statement-I and Statement-II are correct and
Statement-II is not the correct explanation for
Statement-I
(c) Statement-I is correct but Statement-II is incorrect
(d) Statement-I is incorrect but Statement-II is correct
The correct option is (a).
• Statement-I is correct:
o The Government of India decided to
designate 7th August every year as
the National Handloom Day,
inaugurated on 7 August 2015 by
Prime Minister Narendra Modi in
Chennai.
• Statement-II is the correct explanation for
Statement-I:
o The Swadeshi Movement, launched
on 7 August 1905, had encouraged
indigenous industries, particularly
handloom weaving.

o

In recognition of this historic day, the
Government of India declared 7th
August as National Handloom Day to
honour the handloom weavers and
promote the Swadeshi spirit of selfreliance.
Hence, both Statement-I and Statement-II are
correct, and Statement-II is the correct explanation
for Statement-I.
UPSC PYQs - 2024
Q1. With reference to the Government of India Act,
1935, consider the following statements:
1. It provided for the establishment of an All India
Federation based on the union of the British Indian
Provinces, and Princely States.
2. Defence and Foreign Affairs were kept under the
control of the federal legislature.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (a).
• The Government of India Act, 1935 was
passed by the British Parliament and came
into effect in 1937. It was based on a Joint
Select Committee Report led by Lord
Linlithgow.
• Statement 1 is correct:
o The Act proposed the creation of an
‘All India Federation’, consisting of
the British Indian Provinces and the
Princely States.
o However, the federation never came
into being as the princely states did
not join.
• Statement 2 is incorrect:
o Defence and Foreign Affairs were
reserved subjects and were kept
under the control of the GovernorGeneral, not under the federal
legislature.

o

Thus, dyarchy was introduced at the
Centre, while it was abolished at the
provincial level.
• Other key features:
o Abolition of dyarchy in provinces and
introduction of provincial autonomy.
o Establishment of a Federal Court.
o Extension of franchise from 3% to
14% of the population.
o Separate electorates for Muslims,
Sikhs, and others, but not for
Depressed Classes.
o Governor-General retained special
powers such as emergency powers
and veto authority.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
Q2. With reference to revenue collection by
Cornwallis, consider the following statements:
1. Under the Ryotwari Settlement of revenue
collection, the peasants were exempted from revenue
payment in case of bad harvests or natural calamities.
2. Under the Permanent Settlement in Bengal, if the
Zamindar failed to pay his revenues to the state, on or
before the fixed date, he would be removed from his
Zamindari.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) 1 only
(b) 2 only
(c) Both 1 and 2
(d) Neither 1 nor 2
The correct option is (b).
• Statement 1 is incorrect:
o Under the Ryotwari System, the
peasant (ryot) was recognized as the
owner of the land, but he had to pay
land revenue directly to the
government.
o The revenue was fixed in cash,
irrespective of crop output.
o In the event of bad harvests or
natural calamities, the peasants were
not exempted from paying revenue.
o As a result, many peasants fell into
debt traps, borrowing from

moneylenders and eventually losing
their land.
• Statement 2 is correct:
o Under the Permanent Settlement
(introduced in Bengal in 1793) by
Lord Cornwallis, Zamindars were
made hereditary owners of land and
were responsible for collecting and
paying a fixed amount of revenue to
the East India Company.
o The amount was permanently fixed,
regardless of the actual agricultural
yield.
o According to the Sunset Law, if the
Zamindar failed to pay revenue by
sunset of the due date, his Zamindari
could be auctioned off.
Hence, option (b) is correct.

•

In 1814, he founded the Atmiya Sabha to
promote monotheistic ideals of Vedanta and
to campaign against idolatry and caste
rigidity.
• He argued that if reason demanded it, one
could depart from the scriptures.
• This balance of respect for tradition and
advocacy for reform shows that both
statements are correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.

UPSC PYQs - 2025
Q1. Consider the following statements about Raja
Ram Mohan Roy:
1. He possessed great love and respect for the
traditional philosophical systems of the East.
2. He desired his countrymen to accept the rational
and scientific approach and the principle of human
dignity and social equality of all men and women.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) I only
(b) II only
(c) Both I and II
(d) Neither I nor II
The correct option is (c).
• Raja Ram Mohan Roy had a deep
appreciation for the philosophical traditions
of the East, particularly the Upanishads and
Vedanta philosophy.
• He also firmly believed that Indian society
must embrace modern, rational, and
scientific thought to bring about social and
moral regeneration.
• He advocated for a synthesis of Eastern and
Western thought, blending reason with
tradition.

Q2. Consider the following subjects with regard to
the Non-Cooperation Programme:
1. Boycott of law-courts and foreign cloth
2. Observance of strict non-violence
3. Retention of titles and honours without using
them in public
4. Establishment of Panchayats for settling
disputes
How many of the above were parts of the NonCooperation Programme?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) Only three
(d) All the four
The correct option is (c).
• Statement 1 is correct: The movement
included a boycott of government schools,
law courts, and foreign goods.
• Statement 2 is correct: The movement was
based on the strict observance of nonviolence, a fundamental Gandhian principle.
• Statement 3 is incorrect: The program
required the surrender of titles and honours,
not their retention.

•

Statement 4 is correct: The program called for
the establishment of Panchayats and
national schools/colleges as alternatives to
British institutions.
• Thus, three out of the four statements are
correct.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q3. Consider the following statements in respect of
the Non-Cooperation Movement:
1. The Congress declared the attainment of 'Swaraj'
by all legitimate and peaceful means to be its
objective.
2. It was to be implemented in stages with civil
disobedience and non-payment of taxes for the next
stage only if 'Swaraj' did not come within a year and
the Government resorted to repression.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
(a) I only
(b) II only
(c) Both I and II
(d) Neither I nor II
The correct option is (c).
• At the Nagpur Session (1920), the Congress
officially adopted the Non-Cooperation
resolution.
• Statement 1 is correct: The Congress declared
its objective as attainment of Swaraj through
all legitimate and peaceful means.
• Statement 2 is correct: Gandhiji stated that
Swaraj could be achieved within a year if the
program was fully implemented, and that civil
disobedience and non-payment of taxes
would be launched if the government
resorted to repression.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q4. “Sedition has become my religion” was the
famous statement given by Gandhiji at the time of:
(a) the Champaran Satyagraha
(b) publicly violating Salt Law at Dandi
(c) attending the Second Round Table Conference in
London
(d) the launch of the Quit India Movement
The correct option is (b).

•

This statement was made during the Civil
Disobedience Movement, which began with
the Dandi March on March 12, 1930.
• After reaching Dandi, Gandhiji broke the Salt
Law on April 6, 1930, symbolizing resistance
to British oppression.
• In this context, Gandhi declared:
o “The British rule in India has brought
about moral, material, cultural and
spiritual ruination of this great
country... Sedition has become my
religion.”
• This phrase reflected defiance against
colonial injustice and the moral duty to resist
unjust laws.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q5. Who provided legal defence to the people
arrested in the aftermath of the Chauri Chaura
incident?
(a) C. R. Das
(b) Madan Mohan Malaviya and Krishna Kant
(c) Dr. Saifuddin Kitchlew and Khwaja Hasan Nizami
(d) M. A. Jinnah
The correct option is (b).
• After the Chauri Chaura incident (1922), 225
people were arrested and put on trial.
• Although he had left his legal practice, Madan
Mohan Malaviya returned to court to defend
the accused.
• The trial lasted for eight months, initially
sentencing 172 people to death.
• Through Malaviya’s and Krishna Kant’s
efforts, 153 were acquitted, showcasing a
landmark in legal defence and humanitarian
commitment.
Hence, option (b) is correct.
Q6. Subsequent to which one of the following events
did Gandhiji include the upliftment of ‘Harijans’ in
his political and social programme?
(a) The Poona Pact
(b) The Gandhi–Irwin Agreement (Delhi Pact)
(c) Arrest of Congress leadership at the time of the
Quit India Movement

(d) Promulgation of the Government of India Act,
1935
The correct option is (a).
• The Poona Pact (September 1932) was signed
between Mahatma Gandhi and Dr. B. R.
Ambedkar following Gandhi’s fast against the
Communal Award that granted separate
electorates for Depressed Classes.
• After the pact, Gandhi made the eradication
of untouchability a central part of his political
and social program.
• He coined the term ‘Harijans’ (Children of
God) for the Depressed Classes.
• He founded the Harijan Sevak Sangh to work
for their social and economic upliftment.
Hence, option (a) is correct.

Q7. Consider the following fruits:
1. Papaya
2. Pineapple
3. Guava
How many of the above were introduced in India by
the Portuguese in the sixteenth and seventeenth
centuries?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All the three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
• The Portuguese, during the Columbian
Exchange, introduced several New World
crops to India.
• Papaya, pineapple, and guava were all
brought from Central and South America to
India by the Portuguese in the 16th–17th
centuries.

•

These fruits became widely cultivated and
integrated into Indian agriculture and cuisine.
Hence, option (c) is correct.
Q8. Who among the following was the founder of
the ‘Self-Respect Movement’?
(a) ‘Periyar’ E. V. Ramaswamy Naicker
(b) Dr. B. R. Ambedkar
(c) Bhaskarrao Jadhav
(d) Dinkarrao Javalkar
The correct option is (a).
• The Self-Respect Movement was founded in
1925 by E. V. Ramaswamy Naicker, popularly
known as Periyar.
• It aimed to promote rationalism, self-respect,
and social equality, particularly among the
non-Brahmin Dravidian communities of Tamil
Nadu.
• The movement sought to eradicate caste
discrimination, challenge Brahminical
dominance, and promote women’s rights.
• It emphasized rational thinking and rejection
of religious orthodoxy.
Hence, option (a) is correct.
